Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Announces additional
investment in Malaysia
subsidiary
What’s new? Co-Tech’s board today approved an additional NT$1.4bn investment in
its 100%-owned subsidiary, CoTech Development (Penang) Sdn. Bhd., to be financed
with internal funds or bank loans. This follows the company’s September 14
announcement that it would establish a subsidiary in Southeast Asia (note).
Our view: Our latest checks suggest that, due to strong demand from key

customers, the Malaysia plant will primarily produce advanced HVLP copper foil
rather than the RG-series foil originally planned. The new plant is also likely to be
larger than Yunlin Plant III, where Co-Tech plans to add ~600 tons of HVLP3/4
capacity by end-2027. Volume production in Malaysia is unlikely to start before late
2028, so most of the ramp-up should fall in 2029, contributing to that year’s
earnings. On funding, our checks also suggest Co-Tech will finance further

investment in the new plant’s construction and equipment by issuing convertible
bonds.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13 Sep
2026)
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Key Takeaways
We believe Co-Tech's expansion in SEA implies strong multi-year demand and
close partnerships with key customers...
...and does not derail our expectation of a HVLP4 supply deficit from late 2026
onwards.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO)
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity
of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 13%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI demand
n Slower-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Faster-than-expected material upgrades
n Slower-than-expected CPO adoption

Risks to Downside
n Weaker AI demand
n Faster-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Slower-than-expected material upgrades
n Faster-than-expected CPO adoption

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

AVC (3017.TW): Liquid cooling rising penetration and AI servers ramp-up 
to drive growth ahead; Buy
19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

### Page 2

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

矽光子、海外市場帶動營收持續穩健成長 
 維持買進，目標價 750 元：投顧認為隨汎銓竹北的 SAC-TEM 中心
開始貢獻先進製程 MA 收入，加以 AI 晶片及矽光子檢測需求顯著升
溫加持下，汎銓 2026 年營收成長近三成，毛利率提升至 30%以上。
此外，2027 年矽光子分析設備銷售將開始貢獻營收，估計 2026 年
EPS 4.34 元，2027 年 EPS 15.28 元，維持買進，目標價 750 元。 
 預期 3Q26 毛利率大幅優於前期 ，EPS 1.81 元：汎銓 3Q26 營收 7.59
億元 ，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY， 投顧估計毛利率將大幅優於2Q，
3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。 
 AI、矽光子、海外市場、矽光子分析設備等多動能齊發，預期 2027
年營運明顯走升：汎銓今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，
27 年每月營運成本約 2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放
大。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營
收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產，對下半年業務樂觀看待，尤其
海外業績成長極為迅速 ， 預計到今年底 ， 海外業務佔比將有望達將近
40%。另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，
其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國專利。
標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，
標準機台售價約 6,000 多萬元 ， 此設備毛利率預期在50%以上水準，
若額外選配各項高階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。

汎銓 主力為 MA （材料分
析 ） ， 營 收 佔 比超 八 成  
 汎銓成立於 2005 年 7 月，為檢測分析服務公司，協助客戶解決先進製程中遇到
的研發與品質問題，以提高客戶的生產效率，尤其在 AI 晶片、先進封裝、矽光
子領域有深厚布局 ，此外由於材料分析檢測交期平均在三天內、 故障分析檢測交
期在一周內，成為其競爭優勢。以營收比重來分，材料分析（MA）佔 2026 前 7
月營收比重 84％，IC 故障分析（FA）營收佔比 8％、矽光子+AI 晶片營收佔比
8％。 
汎銓有九個 MA 實驗室，三個 FA 實驗室，一個 RA 實驗室。2Q26 台灣營收佔
比 65%， 美國 、 日本 、 大陸等海外營收佔比35%， 目前全球員工人數共869 人。

預期 3Q26 毛利率 大幅 優於 前
期，EPS 1.81 元 
 汎銓 2Q26 營收 6.35 億元，+9.8%QOQ，+16.6%YOY，隨 AI 晶片檢測分析等
高毛利業務需求強勁，毛利率回升至 24.96％，季增 3.4 個百分點，費用率降至
22.4%， 稅後EPS 0.35 元 ， 營業利益金額 1611 萬元 ， 稅後淨利金額 1860 萬元。
汎銓 3Q 營收 7.59 億元，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY，投顧估計毛利率將大幅
優於 2Q，3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。

AI 、 矽 光 子 、 海 外 市 場 、矽
光 子 分 析 設 備 等 多動能 齊
發 ， 預期 20 2 7 年 營運 明顯
走升  
 汎銓表示今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，27 年每月營運成本約
2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放大。今年 3 月起各個新建廠區已
開始貢獻業績，公司運營已從 3 月開始轉虧為盈。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第
三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產 ，對下
半年業務樂觀看待，尤其海外業績成長極為迅速，預計到今年底，海外業務佔
比將有望達將近 40%。投顧預期汎銓 4Q26 營收季增 8.6%，年增 39.2%，估
計 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.8 元。 
另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，其功能即是協助
客戶進行光損及漏光定位，並開發從晶片背面進行收光偵測的技術，鎖定矽光
子常用的 1310nm、1550nm 波段，協助定位 PIC 元件、光耦合及封裝過程中
的異常位置，且其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國
專利。對汎銓更具戰略意義的是，將不再侷限於檢測分析服務供應商的角色，
而是應客戶強烈需求，規劃進一步推出適用於量產端與品質驗證使用的矽光子
測試設備。標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，標準機台
售價約 6,000 多萬元，此設備毛利率預期在 50%以上水準，若額外選配各項高
階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。汎銓近年積極建立 AI 晶片分析
平台，並同步發展矽光子檢測、自主設 備開發與專利授權等新成長動能，期望

術，提供消費者防詐、企業端防詐與金融科技服務。Gogolook 擁有全球最齊全的

whoscall海外市場持續擴張

Gogolook最大的海外市場，其用戶規模、營收貢獻已超越台灣，但仍具備很大的
成長空間；公司於曼谷擁有龐大用戶基礎，目前正進軍泰國二、三線城市。菲律

退休人員及現職公務員(超過120萬名會員)。此合作有助於同步推廣泰國當地B2C、
B2B業務。
⚫ 為了有效控制獲客成本，Gogolook在海外減少依賴成效較低的數位廣告，轉而與
當地政府、銀行及電信商合作。此外，考量到東南亞信用卡普及率較低，

Gogolook在泰國率先推出的「電信帳單扣款」訂閱模式大獲成功，目前此模式也
與台灣遠傳電信合作，藉此攻佔中高年齡層客群。

Gogolook收購ScamAdviser

務，導致ScamAdviser營收下滑，但其實質獲利反而增加。

### Page 12

12
Agent looker
⚫ 因應Agentic AI時代來臨，Gogolook於09/2026推出新產品Agent looker，工作原理
是在AI agent實際造訪或讀取網頁前，即時進行風險檢測並攔截惡意請求，可有效
避免AI agent被詐騙的風險。
⚫ 核心防護機制與功能：(1)毫秒級即時掃描，當AI Agent讀取外部網頁或執行URL
請求時，Agent Looker會在毫秒內對網頁封包與內容進行深度檢查，攔截惡意腳本、
釣魚網站以及針對 AI 模型的提示詞注入(Prompt Injection)攻擊。(2)Gogolook威脅
情資資料庫驅動。系統底層串接 Gogolook 累積多年的全球防詐騙、釣魚網站與風
險網域黑名單。基於海量真實風險樣本的數據庫，使其能快速且精準地辨識異常
行為。(3)活動稽核日誌。提供視覺化的管理儀表板，完整記錄AI Agent的所有外
部網路互動軌跡，包含造訪過的網址、讀取的網頁片段及最終的安全判定結果，
確保AI運作透明且可追溯。
⚫ Agent looker目前處於封閉測試(Private Beta)階段。

資料來源：Gogolook、群益投顧彙整

## Document 262: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

iscal and current account balances can absorb a deterioration
 . 
 I
nflation has increased 
 while 
 currency 
depreciation pressures  
have remained 
 manageable. 
 Yet, 
as 
 the negative impact of high energy prices 
 is expected to persist, 
 V
ietnam

 more

Vietnam’s export base has strengthened further over the past year, notwithstanding the risin
 g 
US tariffs
 , notably 
because

 y 
of the surgin
 g 
global demand for AI
 -

◼ 
 Export growth strengthened in 2025 (+17% in value), driven by electronic goods (+48%) and sales to the U
 nited 
S
tates 
(+28%). V
ietnam 
was 
 one of 
the main targets of President Trump’s tariff war in early 2025, but the tariff shock was much smaller than initially feared, 
and exports were boosted by surgin
 g 
AI
-
 related demand. V
 ietnam  
increased its global market share (1.9% of world exports 
 in 2025 
vs. 1.7% in 2024), supported by gains in the US market (5.7% of US imports vs. 4.2% in 2024) and the semiconductor sector (
 Vietnam 
is estimated  
to 
account for  
2.9% of world exports of semiconductors vs. 1.6% in 2024).  
◼ 
 Solid growth dynamics in industrial 
 out
p
ut 
and exports ha

continued 
 in 2026
 . Exports rose by 
 21.9% y/y in the first eight months 
of the year
 , 
led by the strong increase in both volume and price of tech goods.  
However, 
 Vietnam  
adds little value to the tech goods  
that 
it exports as it speciali
 s
es mostly in assembl

and packaging chips to form electronic components. 
 Therefore, i
 ts balance of 
trade of semiconductor goods is negative
 , as i

◼ 
 V

 ’s trade surplus with the U

 S

has

◼ 
 Meanwhile, V
 ietnam
 ’s imports of Chinese goods have increased rapidly (+3

% y/y in

trade deficit with China to surge.  
Indeed, V
 ietnam  
has played a growing role in the rerouting of Chinese goods to the US. It has also 
benefited largely from the reorgani
 s
ation of production chains in the region and continued to attract

Asian countries. Net FDI inflows have exceeded 4% of GDP in recent years, which has fostered continued expansion of the 
manufacturing sector and progress up the value chain.  
◼ 
 In the 
 short term
 , 
solid export growth dynamics are likely to continue, assuming 
 that

 that

◼ 
 US trade policy remains 
 a source of 
 uncertain
 ty
. The US effective tariff rate on V
 ietnam’s

to 19.3% in Aug

 has fallen

 in 2026 (

 ; it is now close to 10
 %. This 
is higher than

 , 
 but not high enough to undermine the competitiveness of 
 Vietnamese 
 goods 
substantially
 . However, Vietnam is

 and 
 the U

S

 S
tates
.

Vietnam  
is a net energy importer highly exposed to the consequences of the conflict in the Middle East. So far, 
the impact of hydrocarbon supply disruptions has been 
 more moderate  
than expected, but it could 
 worsen 
 in 
the coming months.  
◼ 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to disruptions in energy trade flows
 . I
n 2025, 
 88% of its total crude oil imports came from Kuwait and 49% of 
its gas imports  
were 
from the Gulf region. However, a few factors have attenuated the shock  
resulting from the Iran war
 , as  
V
ietnam  
has reacted swiftly, finding alternative suppliers, introducing measures to contain demand and using its (low) stocks. 
 The country 
produces 70% of its needs for refined products and imports the remainder from Asian countries  
(
though the region’s production is 
dependent on imports from the Gulf countries
 )
. More positively, oil 
 and 
gas account for only 35% of 
 Vietnam
 ’s energy mix (coal: 54% 
and 
renewables: 11%), and the country can part
 ial
 ly switch to coal for electricity generation.  
◼ 
 The potential pass
 -
 through of rising global energy prices to domestic inflation is high. Yet, the government has some fiscal policy 
leeway 
 (
given its moderate deficit and debt ratios
 ) 
and has introduced small measures to contain inflation (

 s

4.

y/y in

,

 , unless inflation accelerates more than expected or depreciation pressures on the dong intensify too much.

VIETNAM HAS NOT YET FIXED ITS EXTERNAL LIQUIDITY PROBLEM
, WHICH IS A SOURCE OF VULNERABILITY 
◼ 
 V
ietnam
 ’s trade balance 
 has 
 deteriorated 
 steadily 
 in 2026. It turned in
 to a deficit  
amounting to 
 USD
19 
bn 
in the first eight months of 
2026 
 (customs data)
 , 
 compared to  
 a surplus 
 in the previous year 
 (
amounting to 
 USD
14 
 bn 
 in the first eight months of 2025
 ).  
This 
 rapid deterioration 
 has resulted notably from the ris
 ing 
import prices of both energy and electronic inputs.  
◼ 
 On the one hand, the current account surplus 
 is 
 decreas
 ing, bu
 t from a comfortable level
 . 
It is projected to narrow 
 to 
2.9
% of GDP in 
2026 from 6.7% in 2025, due to  
the larger energy trade deficit (
 which 
 accounted for  
3.6% of GDP in 2025), the larger trade deficit in 
semiconductors/tech goods, and lower export receipts from tourism 
 (about 
 6% of GDP). 
 Furthermore
 , net FDI  
 inflows will remain 
strong,  
staying close to 4% of GDP in 2026
 . 
Therefore, 
 Vietnam is  
unlikely to need to incur new foreign debt.  
◼ 
 On the other hand, V
 ietnam
 ’s forex reserves are low and do not provide a solid cushion  
 against a deterioration 
 in 
 the balance of 
payments
 . Forex reserves amounted to USD8
 8
.
3 
bn at the end of 
 June 2026
 , which is 
 weak: it is 
 enough 
to service external debt in 
the 
 short ter
 m 
 but covers less than 3 months of imports of goods 
 and 
 services. In 
 particular, i
 f 
 resident capital outflows increase 
(residents’ confidence in the dong 
 may 
be eroded by ri
 s
in
g 
inflation and the external energy shock), currency pressure could intensify 
and the central bank could have difficulties to defend the dong. 
 This risk cannot be ignored
 , 
 even 
 though 
 t
he VND has barely 
depreciated 
 this year (
 the 
central

rate has lost a small 2% against the USD since  
the beginning of 2026
 )
.

MEDIUM
-
TERM PROSPECTS ARE FAVO
U
RABLE 
◼ 
 In the medium term, economic growth is expected to remain strong. It is projected at 5.9% per year on average in 2027
 –
2031, 
compared to  
6.2% in the past decade. This is one of the highest growth rates expected in the ASEAN region.  
◼ 
 This  
projection is based on a rather optimistic scenario, as it assumes that:  
i
) FDI continues to flow in, and the manufacturing export sector expands further
 . This 
 means Vietnam’s external accounts 
 continue 
to 
strengthen in the medium term, thanks to continued 
 current account surpluses
 , 
lower resident capital outflows, declining external 
debt and slowly increasing forex reserves
 ; 
ii
) 
D
emographic trends remain 
 favourable
 , even 
 though they 
 are projected to 
 weaken gradually  
in the medium
 -
 long term
 ;

) 
 Structural r
 eforms make steady progress, helping to improve financial
 -
sector supervision, ease infrastructure constraints in the 
energy and water sectors, boost aggregate productivity (
 i.e. 
 reducing the gap between high
 -
 productivity FDI firms and low
 -
productivity local sectors) and adapt to climate change.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 277: CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22).pdf

### Page 1

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Doanh thu trên các điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu tăng nhờ lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa dự phóng tăng 
11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng trưởng mới, với số điểm kinh doanh AST 2026 dự kiến đạt 145 
điểm và tăng lên 182 điểm vào 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Biên lợi nhuận gộp cải thiện lên 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027 nhờ mở rộng hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ và tối ưu vận hành.

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), 
với động lực: (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ 
VND (+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Biểu đồ 5. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh của AST

Nguồn:AST

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

 Biểu đồ 7. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh AST (tỷ VND)

Nguồn: AST  Nguồn: AST

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá 
mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

## Document 283: DeepSeek全面开源推理框架：兼容英伟达、华为昇腾芯片，单集群日吞吐155万亿Token，每卡每小时收入38美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek全面开源推理框架：兼容英伟达、华为昇腾芯片，单集群日吞吐155万亿Token，每卡每小时收入38美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek 全⾯开源推理框架 DeepInfer 及 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe ，兼容英伟
达与华为昇腾平台。基于该框架， DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 单集群⽇吞吐达 155 万亿 Token ，
按⾕电价计算每卡每⼩时收⼊ 38 美元。通过 Prefill 与 Decode 分离、 KVCache 复⽤及投机
解码等技术， DeepSeek 旨在提升推理效率、降低算⼒成本与能源消耗。
摘要：
DeepSeek 进⼀步开放⼤模型推理基础设施。
继开源⼤模型、 TileLang 编译⼯具及⼀系列推理算⼦库后， DeepSeek 宣布全⾯开源内部推理框
架 DeepInfer ，并同步开源 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe ，旨在帮助开发者和企业优化⼤规
模模型部署，降低 AI 服务的算⼒成本与能源消耗。
DeepSeek 介绍，基于 DeepInfer ，团队已实现 DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 的⾼吞吐、低延迟⼤规模

推理服务。⽬前，单⼀集群的服务吞吐量达到每天 155 万亿 Token ，按⾕电价计算，每卡每⼩时
收⼊为 38 美元。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

推理框架是连接⼤模型能⼒与终端⽤⼾的关键基础设施。随着 AI 应⽤调⽤量增⻓，推理服务的成
本不仅取决于模型本⾝，还受到计算、通信、任务调度及缓存管理等环节的影响。如何在满⾜
延迟要求的同时提⾼有效吞吐，已成为⼤规模 AI 服务降本增效的重要环节。
兼容英伟达与华为昇腾，针对不同推理阶段优化算⼒配置
DeepInfer 集成⾼性能算⼦与深度优化的推理引擎，⽀持分布式推理、推测解码、 SWA Bounded
Replay 及结构化输出等能⼒，可在英伟达与华为昇腾平台运⾏。未来， DeepSeek 新模型的推理
代码及后续性能优化也将在该框架中持续开源。
⾯对⼤规模 AI 调⽤需求， DeepSeek 将推理系统的优化重点放在吞吐量与延迟上。系统采⽤
Prefill 与 Decode 分离部署：前者负责处理输⼊提⽰词，后者负责逐步⽣成 Token 。由于两个阶段
的计算负载不同，分开部署可以独⽴配置资源，分别优化⾸个 Token ⽣成时间和后续 Token ⽣成
间隔。

### Page 3

在硬件组织上， DeepSeek 使⽤常⻅的 8 卡机器承担 Prefill 任务，并采⽤ 64 ⾄ 128 卡超节点承担
Decode 任务。同时，系统通过⾃动扩缩容动态调整两个阶段的实例⽐例，减少流量变化导致的
资源错配；在 MoE 模型推理中，则通过专家并⾏与动态负载均衡，降低不同计算卡之间的负载
差异。
KVCache 最⻓保留 7 天，减少重复计算开销
缓存复⽤是 DeepSeek 降低推理成本的另⼀项关键设计。随着⻓上下⽂对话和 AI Agent 应⽤增
多，相同前缀可能在不同请求中反复出现。如果每次都重新处理这些内容，就会产⽣额外的计
算开销。
DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 将 KVCache 分为 Block KVCache 和 SWA KVCache 两部分，采⽤不同的存
储策略。其中， Block KVCache 存储在独⽴的 3FS 分布式⽂件系统中，相关存储容量约为 100
PiB 。根据 DeepSeek 披露的数据， Agent 流量的 KVCache 保留时间达到 3 天，聊天机器⼈流量则

达到 7 天。
当 后 续 请 求 命 中 已 有 缓 存 时 ， 系 统 可 以 复 ⽤ 此 前 的 计 算 结 果 ， 减 少 重 复 的 Prefill 计 算 。
DeepSeek 披露， 3FS 存储集群的峰值聚合读取带宽达到 10 TiB/s ，峰值聚合写⼊带宽约为 850
GiB/s ，以满⾜⼤规模缓存读写需求。
对于主要在活跃会话内短期复⽤的 SWA KVCache ， DeepSeek 则选择保留在机器内存中，不进
⾏持久化存储。线上数据显⽰，这部分缓存的保留时间约为 180 秒，命中率超过 98% 。
针对 SWA KVCache 未命中的情况， DeepSeek 采⽤ SWA Bounded Replay 机制，仅重算最近
128 个 Token ，并将注意⼒窗⼝限制在重算区间内，以降低缓存缺失带来的额外计算成本。
投机解码提升吞吐，编解码组件同步开源
除缓存复⽤外， DeepSeek 还通过投机解码和异步调度提⾼推理效率。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
DeepSeek 采⽤ DSpark 投机解码框架，根据候选 Token 的预期接受概率动态决定验证范围，避免
将验证算⼒浪费在低接受概率的候选结果上。 DeepSeek 披露，在线部署中，与固定验证 5 个
Token 的⽅式相⽐，变⻓验证使吞吐提升 6% 。
在调度环节，系统通过 CPU 与 GPU 之间的异步协作，让请求调度、前向计算和采样等操作尽可
能重叠，减少设备等待时间。此外， DeepInfer ⽀持结构化输出，可依据 JSON Schema 、正则
表达式等约束控制模型⽣成结果，满⾜特定应⽤对输出格式的要求。
DeepSeek 还同步开源⽣产级 Token 编解码组件 deepseek-recipe 。该组件⽀持 Chat Completions

API 、 Responses API 和 Messages API 等主流接⼝，并通过特殊 Token 边界识别、普通⽂本分段
并⾏编码及流式输出解析等机制，提升⻓⽂本处理效率，确保第三⽅部署的 API 处理逻辑与官⽅
服务保持⼀致。
不过， DeepSeek 并未开放推理系统的全部组件。由于 KVCache 管理和负载均衡部分与内部基础
设施耦合较深，相关代码⽬前尚未完整开源。
从模型、算⼦库到⼤规模推理框架， DeepSeek 此次进⼀步扩展了开源技术栈的覆盖范围。对于
开发者和企业⽽⾔，这有望降低构建私有化及公有云 AI 服务的技术⻔槛；对于⼤模型⾏业⽽⾔，
如何提⾼推理吞吐、复⽤计算结果并适配不同硬件平台，也正成为控制 AI 服务成本的重要⽅向。

## Document 309: FX Daily_ AI jitters have damaged USD safe haven appeal

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ AI jitters have damaged USD safe haven appeal.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

After big moves in front-end USD rates, the dollar has shown it
could be a laggard in further stock market instability if led by AI
concerns. Still, we don’t think we are on the brink of a major
USD decline. EUR/USD may struggle to stay supported today as
protests in France spill over into local assets, while we see
limited impact from US-Ukraine news for now

Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Markets will be closely monitoring AI, France and Ukraine this week
USD: AI jitters leave USD on less solid
ground
AI-related jitters entered the FX picture last week, although
currencies are more accurately borrowing the reaction from the
rates channel. The USD two-year OIS/SOFR has dropped 25bp from
the 14 October post-US CPI print. These are mammoth moves
considering they have been driven almost entirely by AI concerns,
with eﬀectively no macro input.

### Page 2

Markets appear to have a clear reaction function in mind: stock
market turmoil is enough to drastically reduce Federal Reserve
tightening plans, even with inﬂation running hot. The reasoning is
probably linked to the market wealth eﬀect that many believe has
underpinned US economic resilience. This is enough to suggest the
dollar will not be the safe-haven of choice and may actually lag
many other G10 currencies if stock market instability originates in

tech/AI.
Still, contagion to broader US and world equity indices has so far
been contained, while the Nasdaq drop has only erased the last 30
days of gains. AI-related sentiment will remain an input for FX this
week, but could easily fade if tech stocks ﬁnd some stability. From a
macro perspective, despite the repricing lower in US rates, we do
not feel this marks the start of a major USD downtrend. A December
Fed hike remains a likely outcome; policymakers do not appear to

be in a position to send a dovish message at the October meeting,
and still-elevated energy prices argue against a material recovery
in global bond markets.
A return to 101.0 in DXY remains possible over the coming weeks,
but we do not see much more downside beyond that for now. The
US calendar is rather light this week, while the FOMC is in its pre-
meeting communication blackout. For now, we are not observing
any obvious FX impact from souring US-Ukraine (and Europe)

relationships. Markets are probably quite cautious about pricing in
an imminent end to the Russia-Ukraine conﬂict or any longer-term
implications for Europe. Things could change in the case of
meaningful developments this week.
Francesco Pesole

now

has pushed our short-term fair value estimate for EUR/USD higher

to around 1.137-1.139. The current undervaluation is therefore
around 1.3-1.5%, broadly consistent with French ﬁscal risk

anchored around 2.0%.

Today’s focus is Hungary’s draft budget for next year. Hungarian
bonds have rallied strongly in recent weeks on growing
expectations that the plan will target a deﬁcit below 5% of GDP.

However, much of the good news now appears priced in, creating
downside risk if the government falls short of expectations. Even so,
the broader outlook for bonds remains positive, particularly
compared with this year’s 7.0-7.5% deﬁcit, and we expect investors
to buy any dip following a sell-oﬀ.
The region’s ﬁrst economic releases arrive on Tuesday, with Polish
wage, PPI and industrial production data. The main focus, however,
will be the National Bank of Hungary meeting. We expect rates to

remain unchanged at 5.50%, leaving attention on forward
guidance. Although the central bank has identiﬁed the ﬁscal plan
as a key factor for future easing, we believe cuts remain premature
and, global conditions permitting, are unlikely before the end of this
year or next year.
On Thursday, we expect the Central Bank of Turkey to resume its
easing cycle for the ﬁrst time since the US-Iran conﬂict began,

point to downside risks for the economy in the ﬁnal quarter.
Although crowded long TRY positions oﬀer less and less carry, they
remain popular in emerging markets, and we expect this demand
to persist in the coming months despite renewed rate cuts.

developments in Romania, where the new government appears

## Document 321: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 8

### Page 1

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• Indonesia's macro narrative has 
turned more constructive in 
2H26. 
• Key domestic policy concerns 
have eased. 
• Policy alignment has improved. 
• Greater fiscal discipline should 
allow BI to remain focused on 
price stability.  
• The current account is likely to 
widen as the trade surplus 
narrows. 
• Portfolio and FDI inflows should, 
however, narrow the BOP 
shortfall. 
• Few monitorables remain, 
including factors in the 2027 
fiscal math. 
• IDR performance will remain 
heavily influenced by global 
conditions.

INDONESIA: Policy mix aligns

12 OCTOBER 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.

### Page 4

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Next, the direction of travel on the 
contribution of SOE dividends from the 
investment agency to the state coffers 
for 2027 remains unclear, after being 
accounted for this year. Lastly, 
successive stimulus measures in-kind 
and wage subsidies were announced this 
year and could marginally add to the 
strain in 2027 (0.1-0.2% of GDP).

Discussions around the State Finance 
Law also warrant a watch. We maintain 
our deficit forecast at a wider -2.8% to -
2.9% range to account for any revenue 
shortfall or a gradual pace of growth. 
Nonetheless, deficits/debt are likely to 
stay below key thresholds  and not 
emerge as a flashpoint for the markets.

MONETARY POLICY ON AN EVEN 
KEEL

Bank Indonesia has left the benchmark 
rate unchanged  since July 2026, after 
100bp hikes in 1H26. While tighter global 
financial conditions and geopolitical 
risks were key risks on the horizon, the BI 
held the domestic GDP forecast at 4.9 -
5.7% (DBSf: 5.3%) alongside 
manageable inflation, notwithstanding 
El Nino-related risks. Inflation rose to 3.3% 
yoy in Sep26, towards the high end of the 
target range. Authorities have, however, 
assured that the impact of higher food 
prices due to poor weather will be 
addressed through administrative 
measures by regional bodies.

Contained inflation expectations ha ve 
reduced the urgency for BI to tighten 
policy further, allowing policymakers to 
prioritise growth and financial stability 
considerations. The emphasis on 
strengthening the balance of payments 
position reflects continued caution over 
external financing conditions and capital 
flows. However, with the rupiah 
expected to remain stable on the back of 
policy measures and fundamenta ls, the 
BI did not view current external 
pressures as severe enough to warrant 
additional tightening.

BI introduced a host of measures at the 
June to September policy meetings , 
including FX monetary hedging 
instruments and a mechanism to provide 
foreign investors with greater flexibility 
in managing IDR-denominated 
investments in Indonesia.  
These included –  
a) swap hedging per tenor bucket was 
increased to 3M - 15%; 6M - 20%; 12M - 
25%, from 12.5% earlier.  
b) DNDF incentive was increased to 6M - 
25% and 12M – 30%. An increase in FX/ 
NDF swap premium incentives for longer 
tenors will lower hedging costs and

### Page 5

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

encourage longer tenor positions, in turn 
increasing longevity of these flows;  
c) Launch of the VASTRA scheme (FX 
Market Transactions for Hedging 
through Partner Banks) got underway, 
with the naming of 14 domestic partner 
banks. This facility is designed to help 
investors transact with lower underlying 
documentation requirements, tap into 
longer banking hours , and access 
onshore hedging facilities.  
d) deepen FX and money markets by 
expanding Local Currency Transactions 
(LCT) between IDR and CNY , besides a 
few other currencies.

Policymakers are likely to tap a 
combination of administrative and 
macroprudential measures rather than 
solely interest rates to defend the 
currency as well as keep inflationary 
expectations in check.

We expect the pause on rates to extend 
into 4Q26.  This call might be at risk if 
depreciation pressures on the rupiah 
mount again on unfavourable global 
developments and if the US Fed 
undertakes a more aggressive hik ing 
cycle than our baseline (of 75bp hikes).

WIDER CAD WILL BE PARTLY 
OFFSET BY POSITIVE FLOWS

Indonesia’s 1H26 current account 
deficit (CAD) widened significantly  to -
$16bn vs -$3bn in the comparable period 
a year ago. Much of this deterioration 
was led by a narrower goods trade 
balance, while  the services deficit 
widened on the seasonal travel account 
due to the Hajj period. Higher coupon 
payments weighed on the primary 
account.  This pressure is likely to persist 
in 2H as the oil & gas trade deficit 
remains wide and export performance 
has been fi rm but tracking lower than 
regional peers, likely also impacted by 
the El Nino phenomenon in key agri 
commodities. We expect full year CAD to 
be closer to 1.8-2% of GDP from -0.1% in 
2025.

Much rests on the capital flows account. 
While 1H balance of payments (BOP) also 
widened, there was palpable 
improvement in 2Q26 vs 1Q.  2Q BOP 
narrowed significantly to $0.9bn from a 
wide $9.1bn in 1Q, helped by positive 
capital account flows – primarily 
stronger portfolio investment on SRBI 
and debt inflows, and net FDI position. 
This dependency is likely to extend to 2H, 
with capital fl ows till Sep26 holding up 
on account of hedging discounts and 
moves to improve inflows. Global 
conditions, including the narrow ID -US 
yields, however, warrant attention. 
Nonetheless, full-year BOP could remain

### Page 6

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

wide, on a scale comparable to the 2025 
gap.

IN SUMMARY

IDR markets have been relatively 
steady in October despite higher oil 
prices and a bid dollar , though 
sentiment remains sensitive to external 
developments and swings in global risk 
appetite. USDIDR traded within 17600 -
18000, while 10Y yield hovered around 
7.15-7.25%. A jump in the Aug goods 
trade surplus aided this respite, 
alongside foreign purchases of domestic 
debt, which have remained positive this 
month and on a  YTD basis. Insurers, the 
central bank, and retail investors have 
remained key anchors for the bond 
market. SRBI rates are off highs to sub -
7% levels but are attracting steady 
demand, likely encouraged by hedging 
discounts. An improved outlook for flows 
has helped contain spillovers from a 
wider current account deficit. This does 
not take away from the fact that the 
rupiah is amongst the worst performers 
on a year-to-date basis.

Separately, markets will also monitor 
developments in the political economy, 
institutional architecture, and formation, 
as well as the role of state -led agencies 
(e.g., DSI) and progress on the 
centralisation of decision -making. We 
expect the BI to keep on hold, however, 
pressure to tighten policy might return if 
the US Fed hikes rates well into 2027 
and the US dollar benefits from a risk-off 
environment, weighing on the currency.

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持

方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進

且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
